# 📁 MODULE 6 PROJECT: "The Complete Primary-Source Assessment — Mine an Annual Report and Master a Concall"

> **The capstone of primary-source analysis.** You will mine a real Indian company's annual report *and* perform senior-analyst-level concall analysis on its recent earnings calls — preparing properly, decoding the prepared remarks, dissecting the Q&A, tracking guidance and credibility across quarters, reading the tone forensically, and synthesising it all — then cross-check the two sources against each other and produce one verified, forward-looking, evidence-backed investment view. This is the exact primary-source work a professional analyst does, executed end to end.

---

## 🎯 Objective

Extract a clear, evidence-based, forward-looking picture of a real Indian company by mining its latest annual report and performing in-depth, senior-analyst-level analysis of its recent earnings calls — then reading the two sources together as one mutually-checking system. The deliverable is a **complete primary-source assessment** that demonstrates not just that you can read an annual report, but that you can perform concall analysis the way a professional does: preparing, decoding the script, dissecting the Q&A, tracking credibility across quarters, reading the language forensics, and synthesising everything (cross-checked against the audited report) into one verified, forward-looking investment view.

## 🏢 Company Assigned

**Any NSE/BSE-listed company that holds active earnings calls and publishes a detailed annual report** — ideally a large IT, FMCG, capital-goods, banking, or auto name, as these have rich MD&A and active, substantive concalls with engaged analyst Q&A. Choose a company with **at least three or four recent quarterly concall transcripts available** (so you can track guidance and credibility across quarters — central to this project). Concall transcripts and annual reports are available on the company's investor-relations page, and on screener.in and trendlyne.

## To-Do List

**PART A — Mine the Annual Report (the deep, audited foundation)**
1. **Navigate the report.** Download the latest annual report and locate the signal sections — chairman's/MD's letter, MD&A, auditor's report, financial statements, and notes. Skim the boilerplate.
2. **Mine the letters and MD&A.** Summarise the strategy, the main performance drivers, and the key risks management acknowledges. Compare this year's MD&A to last year's — note any language shifts (e.g., "expansion" → "consolidation") and any metrics dropped.
3. **Cross-check a claim against the numbers.** Pick one management claim (e.g., "improved efficiency," "strong growth") and test it against the relevant number (the operating-margin trend, the actual growth rate) — does it hold up, or is it spin?
4. **Read the auditor's report.** Note the opinion type, any Key Audit Matters, and any Emphasis of Matter. Check for any auditor resignation or change.
5. **Interrogate the notes.** Calculate the contingent-liabilities-to-net-worth ratio; identify any related-party transactions of concern; un-blend the company using segment data (compute each segment's margin and identify the strongest and weakest); and note any accounting-policy changes.

**PART B — Master the Concall (senior-analyst-level analysis)**
6. **Prepare for the most recent call.** Before reading the latest concall transcript, note the quarter's results and compute the surprise versus consensus expectations (where it beat or missed). Review the *prior* call to load what management guided and promised. Write down the two or three key questions you'd want the call to answer.
7. **Decode the prepared remarks.** Read the latest call's opening management commentary on two levels — extract the content (performance explanation, strategy, guidance), and decode the curation (which metrics were emphasised vs dropped, how any misses were framed, what was conspicuously absent).
8. **Dissect the Q&A.** Read the Q&A as a cross-examination: identify the key questions analysts asked, classify management's answers (direct / partial / deflected / non-answer), note what management avoided, identify any analyst pile-on (several analysts pressing the same issue), and note whether management was pinned down or escaped into hedges. Identify the single most revealing exchange.
9. **Track guidance and credibility across quarters.** Across the three or four recent calls, compare what management *guided* against what they *delivered* each quarter. Note any quiet walking-back of prior commitments, and whether they own their misses or blame external factors. Form a clear judgement: is this management team's guidance reliable (under-promise/over-deliver) or to be discounted (over-promise/miss)?
10. **Read the tone forensically, and confirm with the numbers.** Across the recent calls, note any language drift, draining confidence, creeping hedging, rising deflection, vagueness, overemphasis, or changed metric definitions. Then *confirm* any suspicion with the accruals ratio — calculate it and state whether the profit management talks up is backed by cash (high quality) or not (a warning).

**PART C — Cross-Check and Synthesise (the two sources as one system)**
11. **Cross-check the call against the report.** Compare what management says on the calls against what the audited annual report shows. Verify the call's key claims against the report's segment data, accruals/cash, and auditor flags. **Hunt for any discrepancy** between the confident narrative and the audited numbers — and flag it, as it's the highest-value signal.
12. **Synthesise into one verified, forward-looking view.** Integrate everything — the report's audited foundation and the call's forward-looking unscripted signals, weighted and cross-checked — into a clear **investment view**: does the combined primary-source evidence confirm, challenge, or change the thesis? State it with specific reasoning on strategy, earnings quality, management credibility, hidden risks, and the forward outlook.

## 📤 Deliverable

A **complete primary-source assessment** (3–5 pages) containing: (Part A) the annual-report findings — the strategy/drivers/risks summary, the claim cross-check, the audit opinion and any flags, the contingent-liability ratio, the segment margins, and any related-party or policy concerns; (Part B) the concall analysis — the preparation and surprise, the prepared-remarks decoding, the Q&A dissection with the most revealing exchange, the cross-quarter guidance/credibility track record, the tone forensics, and the accruals ratio; and (Part C) the cross-check (with any discrepancy flagged) and the synthesised investment view (confirm/challenge/change, with specific reasoning). It should read like real research — evidence-backed, integrated, and concluding in a clear view, not a list of observations.

## ⏱️ Time Estimate

12–18 hours across several sittings (the concall analysis across multiple quarters and the cross-check are substantial). Treat it as the primary-source analysis you'd show an interviewer to prove you can read a report and master a concall like a professional.

## 🏆 What Great Looks Like

1. **The concall analysis is genuinely senior-level, not a transcript summary.** A great submission shows real concall mastery — the preparation and surprise computed, the prepared remarks decoded on two levels (including dropped metrics and framing), the Q&A dissected as a cross-examination (answers classified, the pile-on and the most revealing exchange identified, dodges caught), the guidance and credibility tracked *across multiple quarters* with a clear reliability verdict, and the tone forensics confirmed by the accruals ratio. This — performing concall analysis like a senior analyst from scratch — is the heart of the project, and it should be unmistakably more sophisticated than "here's what management said."

2. **The cross-quarter credibility track record is explicit and used.** The standout assessment doesn't just analyse one call — it tracks said-versus-delivered across three or four quarters, catches any quiet walk-backs, judges whether the team under-promises/over-delivers or over-promises/misses, and *uses that credibility judgement* to weight the current guidance. This quarter-by-quarter tracking is what separates senior concall analysis from a single-call read.

3. **The two sources are cross-checked, and any discrepancy is flagged.** The best submissions read the report and call as one system — verifying the call's claims against the report's audited numbers and *hunting for discrepancies* between what management says and what their verified figures show. A flagged discrepancy (a "robust" segment the notes show weak, an emphasised profit the accruals show isn't cash, an audit flag the call ignored) — or a clear, evidenced finding that the two sources are consistent — demonstrates genuine primary-source mastery, because this cross-check is invisible to anyone reading one source.

4. **The tone forensics are confirmed by the numbers, never relied on alone.** A great submission reads the language forensically (drift, draining confidence, hedging, changed definitions) but treats it as a *suspicion to confirm* — validating it with the accruals ratio and the cash, never concluding from "vibes" alone. This shows the discipline of combining language's earliness with data's reliability.

5. **It ends in a clear, integrated investment view — not a list.** The assessment must conclude with a synthesised confirm/challenge/change judgement, integrating the report and the call, with specific reasoning on strategy, earnings quality, management credibility, hidden risks, and the forward outlook — and ideally noting what would change the view and what to watch next quarter. It reads like real research that *concludes*, weighing all the evidence (cross-checked across both sources) into one verified, forward-looking conviction — the difference between a primary-source *analysis* and a primary-source *summary*.

---

*Module 6 complete. You can now navigate a 300-page annual report to its signal sections in under an hour, mine the letters and MD&A for strategy and tells, decode the auditor's report, and interrogate the notes for the truth the headline numbers conceal — and, above all, you can perform concall analysis like a senior analyst from scratch: preparing properly before a call, decoding the prepared script on two levels, reading the Q&A as the cross-examination it is, asking (and recognising) the sharp questions that pin management down, tracking guidance and management credibility across quarters, reading the forensics of tone and language and confirming them with the numbers, and synthesising everything into a clear investment view. And you can read the annual report and the concall as one mutually-checking system, cross-checking the two to catch the discrepancies between what management says and what their audited numbers show — the highest-value signal in primary-source analysis. The primary sources are freely available to everyone; reading them this way — deeply, forensically, and as one integrated system — is what almost no one does, and it's the foundation of genuine, differentiated equity research. The documents that intimidate everyone else are now your edge.*
